--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

| count | drug trial counts included retired trials | counts join live Trial nodes |
| should-fix | ordering wording, ring cut silently, stale trail after errors, out-of-order replies, ambiguous names silent, action/class labels, omitted details, malformed props, `javascript:` URLs, "All three" | each group states its actual sort; ring says drawn/total; trail cleared on refusal/not-found/error; replies older than the latest request are dropped; the reading and other matches (company, target, refused span) are shown; stored action codes shown with a Korean gloss; sponsor class code shown; centre start date, drug count and phase always shown; props that are not objects read as empty; only clinicaltrials.gov links are clickable; docstring fixed |

words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never
BEVACIZUMAB), M4-B2 (no distance cap), M4-B3 (one pass, no pre-consumed qualifier) and the EGFR/AstraZeneca over-refusal
are covered by tests. Tests 326.

# Independent relation-map review — round 4

Reviewed HEAD: `bf436c6e93c3eef9e115159ede951a5ecaee5472`, 2026-11-03. Immediate before/after comparison: `574097f` → `bf436c6`; accumulated repair review: `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD`. Specification: the user's round-4 brief, original approval bar, [m1](../m1/REPORT.md), [m2b](../m2b/REPORT.md), [m3](../m3/REPORT.md), and `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.

**Approval remains blocked.** The exact named m3 blockers are fixed, but the new shortest-span rule can bind a qualifier to the wrong drug and map the refused product's originator. The six-token limit also admits a qualified multi-token drug alias that the previous version refused. Separately, the original single-token qualifier pass still prevents a refusal from reaching its drug. These are current-database map and QA reproductions, not malformed-data fixtures or external pharmacology assumptions.

## Specification blockers

### M4-B1 — P1, new regression: equal-length binding refuses the wrong drug

Exact unchanged-server request:

```text
POST /api/explore
{"text":"osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab"}
```

HTTP 200 contains:

```json
{"center":{"id":"CHEMBL1201583","label":"BEVACIZUMAB","trials":122},
 "also":[{"refused":"osimertinib with generic version"}]}
```

This is an excerpt of the full response in [attack_http.json](attack_http.json). With the exact previous linker and previous `_JOINERS`, the centre is **OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410** and the refused span is **generic version of bevacizumab**. The current full-string resolver returns `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no drug ID. Reversing the drugs (`bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib`) also selects the qualified second drug. Adding just `a` — `osimertinib with a generic version of bevacizumab` — changes the current result back to OSIMERTINIB. This is a distance/tie artifact, not a product-identity distinction.

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Previous linker's first evidence | Current HTTP first evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

Both current answers have 12 evidence rows; status/evidence-count checks alone miss the subject switch. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact before/after QA](names_final_checks.json).

**Cause:** `with` was removed from `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:91-95`). `_drug_spans` supplies the first drug before the second (`222-239`). Both `osimertinib with generic version` and `generic version of bevacizumab` are four-token candidate spans. The shortest-span selector iterates drug spans in that order and updates only on strictly shorter length (`255-272`), so the first candidate wins. Its qualifier tokens enter `out`; the correct second candidate is then disallowed by `span & out` (`264`). BEVACIZUMAB remains acceptable at `181-189`, and the map takes it at `src/app.py:88-95`.

Additional m3 controls `osimertinib; generic version of bevacizumab` and `osimertinib， generic version of bevacizumab` change from a whole-input refusal to a BEVACIZUMAB centre. Their separators are not clause boundaries, so the shortest-span algorithm consumes the left drug and leaves the qualified right drug available. The previous over-refusal was a usability failure; the new originator leak is a blocking regression. [Exact comparisons](names_separator_checks.json).

**Required outcome:** bind the qualifier to the product it describes without consuming a separately named valid drug. This input must never map or answer for generic bevacizumab as BEVACIZUMAB. Preserve the repaired albumin-bound paclitaxel case; globally reinstating `with` as a separator would reopen m3-B1.

### M4-B2 — P1: six-token cap admits qualified longer aliases and distant qualifiers

`POST /api/explore {"text":"generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid"}` now draws **PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX / CHEMBL2108628**, with `also:[]`. The same full input is `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` at `/api/resolve`; previous `574097f` refuses with no centre. This exact string was already a m3 supplementary control. [Full current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact previous/current comparison](names_attack.json).

**Cause:** the stored alias occupies five tokens. `_drug_spans` deliberately retains that full non-overlapping name (`src/query.py:222-239`), but `_qualified_drug_spans` only examines spans up to **six tokens** (`258`: `range(b - a, 7)`). The qualifier plus `of` plus the full alias requires eight. No tested span can contain both `generic version` and the complete drug, so nothing is refused. The map then accepts the bare full alias inside the refused product description. This is a new regression even though the bare poliglumex alias correctly remains supported.

The cap also leaves a simpler inherited bypass: `generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab` (seven tokens) draws BEVACIZUMAB with `also:[]`; the six-token control `generic version of the antibody bevacizumab` refuses. QA `Which trials evaluate generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab?` returns 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows. `What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?` also returns current evidence for CHEMBL2108628 (12 rows, first ACTS_ON edge), despite the qualified product being unresolved. [HTTP responses](attack_http.json).

The **plain qualified phrase** is a new map/QA regression: previous refusal/0 evidence becomes a centre/7 profile-mechanism rows. The longer `What targets ... act on?` question already bypassed the previous linker (12→12 rows), as did `Which trials evaluate generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid?` (4→4). Those prefixed-question failures are inherited. [Exact before/after checks](names_final_checks.json), [plain-phrase comparison](names_attack.json).

**Required outcome:** an arbitrary token-distance ceiling must not turn a known qualified product into a positive originator match. Preserve valid bare multi-token aliases and fail safely when qualification cannot be bound reliably.

### M4-B3 — P1, inherited: the single-token prepass blocks its own extension to the drug

| Exact `/api/explore` text | Current result | Current `/api/ask` reproduction |
|---|---|---|
| `biosimilar version of bevacizumab` | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583; only `biosimilar version` refused | `Which trials evaluate biosimilar version of bevacizumab?` → 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows |
| `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463; only `liposomal formulation` refused | `Which trials evaluate liposomal formulation of doxorubicin?` → 3 DOXORUBICIN trial rows |

The existing resolver refuses both complete products. Both parent leaks also occur with `574097f`, so these are **inherited**, not attributed to the latest repair. They nevertheless defeat the approval bar and the new claim that refusal is bound to its drug. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [before/after](names_attack.json).

**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-151` marks the qualifier and immediate neighbour before binding. `_drug_spans(tokens, refused)` skips those tokens (`252`, `225-229`), and every candidate crossing them is rejected by `span & refused` (`264`). Thus the helper cannot extend a previously recognized qualifier across `version of` or `formulation of` to its drug. These examples fit within six tokens; raising only the cap does not fix them.

The two-product input `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin and biosimilar version of bevacizumab` links **both parent drugs**, selects DOXORUBICIN and puts BEVACIZUMAB in `also`. By contrast, adjacent `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` correctly refuses both. [Two-product controls](names_attack.json). The m3 hyphenated control `paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles` also still leaks PACLITAXEL: its one-token qualifier cannot extend across the pre-refused neighbour `with`. The unhyphenated fixed example is therefore not a general solution. [Exact prior control](names_new_boundaries.json).

**Required outcome:** single-token and multi-token qualification need one consistent binding process; already refused qualifier tokens must not make their own qualified drug available as a positive hit.

## No-relink and question-answering assessment

The refusal blockers above reproduce even when matched spans may be relinked in an otherwise identical HEAD linker. They are not caused by the no-relink repair.

- All **7 actual demo questions**, sent over HTTP, have complete response objects identical to the original `bfcb83d` query module. They are also identical to immediate predecessor `574097f` in the focused QA comparison. [HTTP demo evidence](demo_results.json), [focused comparison](names_attack.json).
- Across **2,584 unique multi-token alias keys**, 402 entity lists change; none changes the first accepted identity or introduces a new Drug. The common change removes an embedded parent Drug/company name. Bare salt/full-name identity is preserved. [Alias comparison](names_attack.json).
- The isolated counterfactual uses HEAD's exact helpers, dictionaries and data, and changes only `(refused | matched)` back to `refused` in the overlap guard (`src/query.py:168`). **1,648 questions**: 28 completely identical, 1,596 entity-metadata-only changes, 24 semantic changes, 0 exceptions. It covers the **400 alias keys changed specifically by this toggle × four QA templates, plus 48 company/target/mixed controls**. This differs from the 402 aliases changed by the full previous-to-current repair comparison above. [Reproducer](qa_relink_sweep.py), [all inputs and changed answers](qa_relink_sweep.json).
- The 24 semantic changes remove a Company read from inside a stored full Drug alias, switching `company_portfolio` to `drug_profile`. For example `Tell me about GSK 1120212` previously used nested GSK and returned 12 GlaxoSmithKline portfolio rows; current returns TRAMETINIB's three profile/mechanism rows. `Tell me about EPOETIN BETA` stops treating BETA as company `ORG:beta`. These changes agree with the stored full aliases; they are not adverse regressions found by this review.
- Explicit `What trials evaluate erlotinib hydrochloride and erlotinib?` still links both separately named records. Multi-entity target/company questions and mixed accepted/refused inputs were checked separately; passing demo/salt checks do not excuse the subject-switch failures above.

**Conclusion for this isolated change:** no adverse no-relink QA regression was found in the exercised corpus. This is a bounded result, not proof for every possible sentence.

## Should-fix items and remaining successful checks

1. **Inherited over-refusal of a neighbouring target/company.** `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` refuse the entire input; the reversed forms retain EGFR/AstraZeneca. The generic-version equivalents now retain the target/company. This follows the unconditional immediate-neighbour rule at `src/query.py:149-151`; it does not identify the neighbour's type or syntactic role. A `with` between the target/company and qualifier also preserves them. No invented edge is needed for this usability failure. [Order comparisons](names_attack.json), [HTTP with controls](attack_http.json).
2. **Unchanged qualifications:** unsupported lowercase IDs/Investigator names, raw malformed-request errors, empty-form retention and numeric Infinity are marked NOT FIXED above. None is treated as a new repair regression or an invalid numeric value actually drawn.
3. **Stored graph guarantees hold in the checked snapshot.** Seven retired nodes, 142 retired edges, zero live edges with retired endpoints, no self-loops. All six node types are covered. PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES, ACTS_ON and role-aware SPONSORS match their endpoints. Trial details, sponsor count/class, Drug global count/max phase and actions match stored values. [Exhaustive evidence](db_validate.json), [snapshot facts](db_snapshot_facts.json).
4. **Chunking and size:** the 1,001-Drug fixture crosses three count batches with count/weight 1 for every item. Disease MONDO_0005233 returns 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 72.117 ms** in the recorded call. **24 concurrent requests / 12 workers**, all expected 200 shapes, **598.259 ms** total. These are local samples, not load benchmarks. [Fixtures](db_fixtures.json), [HTTP](http_results.json).
5. **No writes or model calls on exercised application paths.** Explore issues reads; `Engine.ask` explicitly uses `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). The app does not globally open SQLite read-only, so this is a path/integrity observation rather than a database-permission guarantee.

## Standards axis and integrity

No tracked AGENTS/CONTRIBUTING/CODING/docs/agents standards source was found. **Standards findings: 0 documented violations.** User evidence/no-tracked-edit instructions were followed. KOA Studio / KhOpenApi code-change comparisons do not apply because no application code was changed. [Separate standards review](standards_findings.md).

All review-created files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m4/`; pre-existing `brief.md` and the live session `log.txt` were not edited by audit scripts. No tracked/staged diff was introduced. Real DB SHA-256 remains `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`. Audit-owned servers on 8880 and 8881 are closed. [Final integrity](integrity.json), [main server](http_lifecycle.json), [supplemental server](supplemental_http_lifecycle.json), [attack server](attack_http_lifecycle.json), [browser-test server](browser_lifecycle.json).

**Specification findings: 3 blocking mechanisms — two new regression mechanisms plus an inherited qualifier-extension bypass — and the separately listed should-fix qualifications.** The exact old named m1/m2b/m3 blockers pass; four m3 supplementary inputs develop originator leaks (the two punctuation cases previously refused too broadly). # Relation map — Codex review 1 (2026-11-03 KST)

Report: `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`. Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED. Every finding reproduced before fixing.

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | "bevacizumab - awwb", "[89Zr] trastuzumab" drew the originator (linker joined tokens without the refusal checks) | `link_entities` refuses a multi-token window that names a distinct product or tracer, and none of its tokens link (fixes questions too) |
| B2 | a collaborator was labelled the trial's lead sponsor | SPONSORS groups keep `role`; headings say lead sponsor or collaborator |
| B3 | an investigator was labelled an institution | heading uses 연구자 for Investigator groups |
| B4 | a retired node could be explored by name | the centre must be live whatever path resolved it |
| count | drug trial counts included retired trials | counts join live Trial nodes |
| should-fix | ordering wording, ring cut silently, stale trail after errors, out-of-order replies, ambiguous names silent, action/class labels, omitted details, malformed props, `javascript:` URLs, "All three" | each group states its actual sort; ring says drawn/total; trail cleared on refusal/not-found/error; replies older than the latest request are dropped; the reading and other matches (company, target, refused span) are shown; stored action codes shown with a Korean gloss; sponsor class code shown; centre start date, drug count and phase always shown; props that are not objects read as empty; only clinicaltrials.gov links are clickable; docstring fixed |

## Round 3 (audit_evidence_claims/m3) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| # | Finding | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M3-B1 | "with" as a joiner let "paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles" draw PACLITAXEL (and QA answer with it) | refusal is now bound to the drug it qualifies: the shortest span holding that drug and no other drug, inside one list item; "with" is no longer a joiner |
| M3-B2 | "What is the generic version of bevacizumab?" drew BEVACIZUMAB (inherited) | same repair: the span must contain the drug, so the question prefix no longer absorbs the qualifier |
| should-fix | the single-token qualifier pass crossed commas ("osimertinib, liposomal doxorubicin" refused osimertinib) | neighbours are taken only within the same list item and never a joining word |
| found while fixing | words of a longer name were read again ("erlotinib hydrochloride" also listed ERLOTINIB) | a matched span is not re-linked |

## Round 4 (audit_evidence_claims/m4) — CHANGES REQUIRED

Shortest-span binding kept failing (wrong drug on ties, a six-token reach, qualifiers already consumed by the token pass).
Replaced by a fail-closed rule: text is cut into list items at , ; ， ； 、 and listing words; an item in which any run of
words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never
BEVACIZUMAB), M4-B2 (no distance cap), M4-B3 (one pass, no pre-consumed qualifier) and the EGFR/AstraZeneca over-refusal
are covered by tests. Tests 326.

    return {'exit_code': proc.returncode,

          for name in initial['hashes']}

    with socket.socket() as sock:
        ports[str(port)] = sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1', port)) != 0

missing = [link for link in links
           if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://'))
           and link != 'integrity.json' and not (OUT / link).exists()]

"""Round 4 HTTP and QA regression checks; all output stays beside this file."""
import ast
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import types
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
PORT = 8880
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
os.environ["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"

def git(*args):
    p = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": p.returncode, "stdout": p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": p.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

def post(path, payload):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        res = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        res = e
    return {"status": res.status, "body": json.loads(res.read())}

before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
initial = {"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"), "status": git("status", "--short"),
           "db_sha256": before}
(OUT / "initial_integrity.json").write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
with socket.socket() as s:
    assert s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port already occupied"

log = (OUT / "server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8")
proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
                        env=os.environ.copy(), stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
try:
    for _ in range(100):
        if proc.poll() is not None:
            raise RuntimeError("server exited")
        try:
            urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
            break
        except OSError:
            time.sleep(.1)
    else:
        raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
    # Re-run every original HTTP case without executing a writer in m1.
    original = (ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims/m1/http_audit.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    adapted = original.replace("8876", str(PORT)).replace("bfcb83d", "bf436c6")
    (OUT / "http_audit.py").write_text(adapted, encoding="utf-8")
    run = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "http_audit.py")], cwd=ROOT,
                         capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"})
    (OUT / "http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
    assert run.returncode == 0

    regression = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "regression_http.py")],
        cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING":"utf-8"})
    (OUT / "regression_http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(regression.stdout + regression.stderr)
    assert regression.returncode == 0

    # Load the previous linker/answer module in memory; use the identical database.
    old = types.ModuleType("m2b_old_query")
    old.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
    sys.modules[old.__name__] = old
    code = subprocess.run(["git", "show", "bfcb83d:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT,
                          capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode("utf-8")
    exec(compile(code, old.__file__, "exec"), old.__dict__)
    baseline = old.OncoKG()
    baseline.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
    html = (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    questions = json.loads(re.search(r"const EX2 = (\[.*?\]);", html, re.S).group(1))
    # Verify CLI/dashboard demos are covered by the page list, without running
    # their entrypoints (which write tracked report/dashboard artifacts).
    sources = {}
    for filename, variable in [("src/query.py", "questions"),
                               ("src/make_dashboard_data.py", "demo_questions")]:
        parsed = ast.parse((ROOT / filename).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        assignments = [n for n in ast.walk(parsed) if isinstance(n, ast.Assign)
                       and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == variable for t in n.targets)]
        value = assignments[-1].value
        if isinstance(value, ast.BoolOp):
            value = value.values[-1]
        sources[filename] = ast.literal_eval(value)
        assert set(sources[filename]) <= set(questions)
    answers = []
    for question in questions:
        previous = baseline.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
        current = post("/api/ask", {"text": question})
        answers.append({"question": question, "http_status": current["status"],
                        "identical_to_bfcb83d": current["body"] == previous,
                        "current": current["body"], "previous": previous})
    baseline.conn.close()
    (OUT / "demo_results.json").write_text(json.dumps({"source_lists": sources,
        "answers": answers}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps({"demo_questions": len(answers),
        "all_http_200": all(a["http_status"] == 200 for a in answers),
        "all_identical_to_bfcb83d": all(a["identical_to_bfcb83d"] for a in answers)}))
finally:
    proc.terminate()
    proc.wait(timeout=10)
    log.close()
    with socket.socket() as s:
        port_closed = s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
    after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    (OUT / "http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
        "server_exit": proc.returncode, "port_closed": port_closed,
        "database_unchanged": after == before, "db_sha256_before": before,
        "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
"""Own an unchanged CLI server; independently verify round-3 name and UI cases."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
PORT = 8880
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"


def post(path, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    return {"path": path, "text": text, "status": response.status,
            "body": json.loads(response.read())}


before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port occupied"
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"}
with (OUT / "attack_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        inputs = [
            "osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab",
            "bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib",
            "osimertinib with a generic version of bevacizumab",
            "generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid",
            "Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid",
            "biosimilar version of bevacizumab",
            "liposomal formulation of doxorubicin",
            "generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab",
            "generic version of the antibody bevacizumab",
            "generic version of bevacizumab and generic version of trastuzumab",
            "EGFR with biosimilar bevacizumab",
            "AstraZeneca with biosimilar bevacizumab",
        ]
        cases = [post(path, value) for value in inputs
                 for path in ("/api/explore", "/api/resolve")]
        for value in [
            "Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?",
            "Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?",
            "What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?",
            "Which trials evaluate biosimilar version of bevacizumab?",
            "Which trials evaluate liposomal formulation of doxorubicin?",
            "Which trials evaluate generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab?",
        ]:
            cases.append(post("/api/ask", value))
        (OUT / "attack_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(
            {"base": BASE, "cases": cases}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
        for case in cases:
            body = case["body"]
            print(json.dumps({"path": case["path"], "text": case["text"], "status": case["status"],
                "center": body.get("center", {}).get("id"), "refused": body.get("refused"),
                "evidence_count": len(body.get("evidence", [])),
                "first_edge": (body.get("evidence") or [{}])[0].get("edge_id")}, ensure_ascii=False))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        (OUT / "attack_http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "server_exit": proc.returncode,
            "port_closed": closed, "database_unchanged": before == after,
            "db_sha256_before": before, "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        assert closed and before == after
"""Grounded question answering over the graph.

The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.

Three properties the answering layer holds to:

* **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
  each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
* **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
  the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
  against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
  graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
  directly.
* **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
  reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T

DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# evidence
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass
class Evidence:
    statement: str
    edge_id: str
    relation: str
    source: str
    source_record: str | None
    fetched_at: str | None
    confidence: float
    tier: str | None = None
    url: str | None = None
    detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)

    def citation(self) -> str:
        base = self.source_record or self.source
        return f"[{base}]"


@dataclass
class Answer:
    question: str
    intent: str
    entities: list[dict]
    text: str
    evidence: list[Evidence]
    generated_by: str
    checks: dict

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "question": self.question,
            "intent": self.intent,
            "entities": self.entities,
            "answer": self.text,
            "generated_by": self.generated_by,
            "checks": self.checks,
            "evidence": [
                {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
                 "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
                 "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
                 "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
                for e in self.evidence
            ],
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# engine
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
# them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
# nanoparticles").
_JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
            "or/and", "and/or"}


class OncoKG:
    def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
                 resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
        self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
        self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
        self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
        self.disease = resolved["disease"]
        self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
                        if t.get("symbol")}
        # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
        # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
        self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
        for row in self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
            self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
            self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
        # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
        for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
            if same in self.companies:
                self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
        self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}

    # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
    def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
        found: list[dict] = []
        seen: set = set()
        tokens: list[str] = []
        clause: list[int] = []          # which comma/semicolon-separated piece each token is in
        for ci, piece in enumerate(re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question)):
            for t in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
                t = t.strip(" ?.,!;:")
                if t:
                    tokens.append(t)
                    clause.append(ci)

        # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
        # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
        # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
        # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
        # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
        # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
        # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
        # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
        seg: list[int] = []
        n = 0
        for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
            if t.lower() in _JOINERS:
                n += 1
                seg.append(-1)
                continue
            if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
                n += 1
            seg.append(n)
        refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
                             if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
        drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0})
        for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
            idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
            text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
            # some patterns are anchored ("[89Zr] trastuzumab"), so every run of words
            # in the item is tried, not only the whole item
            if not any(T.is_distinct_product(w) or T.is_radiotracer(w)
                       for w in (" ".join(tokens[x] for x in idx[i:j])
                                 for i in range(len(idx)) for j in range(i + 1, len(idx) + 1))):
                continue
            hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
            if not hit and not (refused & set(idx)):
                continue                        # a qualifier with no drug: nothing to refuse
            refused.update(hit)
            found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
                          "matched_text": text,
                          "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
                                 "the originator molecule"})

        matched: set[int] = set()
        for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
            for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
                if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
                    continue
                window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
                if len(window) < 3:
                    continue
                key = T.canonical_key(window)

                hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
                # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
                # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
                # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
                if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
                    hits = None
                if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
                    did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
                    if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Drug", did))
                        found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
                                      "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
                                      "matched_text": window,
                                      "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
                    matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
                    continue

                if window.lower() in self.targets:
                    t = self.targets[window.lower()]
                    if ("Target", t["target_id"]) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Target", t["target_id"]))
                        found.append({"type": "Target", "id": t["target_id"],
                                      "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
                    matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
                    continue

                if key in self.companies:
                    cid = self.companies[key][0]
                    if ("Company", cid) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Company", cid))
                        row = self.conn.execute(
                            "SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (cid,)).fetchone()
                        found.append({"type": "Company", "id": cid,
                                      "label": row["label"], "matched_text": window,
                                      "also_matching_ids": self.companies[key][1:]})
                    matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
                    continue

                if key in self.disease["index"]:
                    if ("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]))
                        found.append({"type": "Disease",
                                      "id": self.disease["disease_id"],
                                      "label": self.disease["name"],
                                      "matched_text": window})
        return found

    def _drug_spans(self, tokens: list[str], skip: set[int]) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
        """Token spans that name exactly one drug, longest first, not overlapping."""
        spans: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
        taken: set[int] = set(skip)
        for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
            for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
                if taken & set(range(i, i + size)):
                    continue
                window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
                if len(window) < 3:
                    continue
                hits = self.dic["index"].get(T.canonical_key(window))
                if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
                    hits = None
                if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
                    spans.append((i, i + size))
                    taken.update(range(i, i + size))
        return spans

    def org_family(self, company_id: str) -> list[str]:
        """The organisation plus any companies a curated parent edge links to it."""
        ids = {company_id}
        for row in self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT dst FROM edge WHERE rel='PARENT_OF' AND src=?"
                " AND retired_at IS NULL", (company_id,)):
"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
        the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
        mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
        page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
        sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
        keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        also: list = []
        matched = None
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (text,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if not hits:
                if refused:
                    return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
                return {"not_found": text}
            nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
            also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
            for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
                lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (oid,)).fetchone()
                if lab and oid != nid:
                    also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
            # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
            # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
            key = T.canonical_key(text)
            for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
                if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
                    lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                       (cid,)).fetchone()
                    if lab:
                        also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
            t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
            if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
                also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
            also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
        node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
                            "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
        if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
            return {"not_found": text}
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
            "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
            "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
            "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
            (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
        drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
        trials = {}
        for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
            chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
            trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
                "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
                "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
                f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
        groups: dict = {}
        for r in rows:
            ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
            item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
            role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
            if r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
                item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
            if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
                n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
                # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
                item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
                item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
                item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
            elif r["type"] == "Trial":
                item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
                item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
            elif r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["weight"] = item["trials"]
            elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
                score = ep.get("association_score")
                item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
                item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
            else:
                item["weight"] = 0
            if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
                item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
            if role:
                item["role"] = role
            key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
            groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
        out = []
        for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
            out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
                        "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
        out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
        props = _obj(node["props"])
        return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
                           "matched_text": matched,
                           "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
                           "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
                                     ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
                                      "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
                "also": also, "groups": out}

    def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
        return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()

    def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
        """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
        res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
        res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
        return res

    def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
        """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
        checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
                                       vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                       linker=kg.link_entities)
        return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
                "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
                              "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence)}


def _number(v) -> bool:
    return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v


def _obj(raw) -> dict:
    """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
    try:
        v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
    except ValueError:
        return {}
    return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
                if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
                    raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
                                     "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/explore":
                        res = engine.explore(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/structured":
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.structured(text, answer)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.check(text, answer)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
                                           allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")
            except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
                self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass
    return Handler


def main() -> None:
    port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
    engine = Engine()
    print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
    ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

"""Surface-form cleaning rules.

Every rule is named and returns the name when it fires, so a resolution can be
explained after the fact ("this matched only after the salt form was stripped").
That traceability is the point: an unexplainable match cannot be reviewed, and an
unreviewable pipeline cannot be trusted with reference data.

# A category pattern, rather than an exact category string. A head noun like
# "inhibitor" or "blockade" qualified by a gene or pathway is a class, whatever
# the qualifier is, so the exact-membership set above can never be complete.
RE_CLASS_PATTERN = re.compile(
    r"^(?:[A-Za-z0-9\-/\s]{2,90}?)\s*"
    r"(?:inhibitors?|blockades?|antagonists?|agonists?|modulators?|"
    r"chemotherapy|chemotherapies|immunotherapy|immunotherapies|"
    r"radiotherapy|regimens?|doublets?|triplets?|backbones?|"
    r"antibod(?:y|ies)|conjugates?|vaccines?|inhibition)$",
    re.I,
)

# Words that make the pattern above a *product* rather than a class: a named
# molecule qualified by a form word is still that molecule. The stem has to
# end a word *with a real prefix in front of it*. Written as a whole-word
# match on the stem itself, this exempted only the literal word "mab": it let
# "mab inhibitor" through as a product and blocked "pembrolizumab antibody"
# as a class.
RE_CLASS_PATTERN_EXEMPT = re.compile(
    r"\b[a-z]{3,}(?:mab|nib|tinib|ciclib|parib|zomib|sertib|degib|rafenib)\b", re.I)


def is_class_like(text: str) -> bool:
    """True for a category description that no single molecule can satisfy."""
    t = (text or "").strip()
    if not t or RE_CLASS_PATTERN_EXEMPT.search(t):
        return False
    return bool(RE_CLASS_PATTERN.match(t))


# A biosimilar, a nanoparticle/liposomal/pegylated reformulation, or a
# sponsor-suffixed version is a separate regulatory product from the
# originator. The molecule name is the same; the asset is not.
RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT = re.compile(
    r"\bbio[\s\-]?similar\b"
    r"|\bbio[\s\-]?betters?\b"
    r"|\bfollow[\s\-]?on\s+biologic\b"
    r"|\bnab[\s\-]"
    r"|\bnanoparticle\s+albumin[\s\-]?bound\b"
    r"|\balbumin[\s\-]?bound\b"
    r"|\bnano[\s\-]?particulat\w*\b|\bnanoparticle\s+paclitaxel\b"
    r"|\boral\s+azacitidine\b"
    r"|\bliposomal\b|\bpegylated\b|\bpeg(?:ylated)?[\s\-]r?h?[a-z]{1,6}\b"
    r"|\bgeneric\s+version\b"
    r"|\b(?:injection|solution|product)\s+of\s+[a-z]+mab\b",
    re.I,
)

# "Bevacizumab-Pfizer", "Trastuzumab-dkst": a hyphenated sponsor name or an
# FDA four-letter suffix on an INN stem.
RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX = re.compile(
    r"^[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|tinib|ase|grastim|stim|poetin)\s*[\-–]\s*(?P<suffix>[A-Za-z]{2,12})$", re.I)

# Words that follow a drug name after a hyphen but are not suffixes at all.
# "pembrolizumab-free" is an absence, not a product.
SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES = {"free", "only", "like", "base", "type", "form", "dose",
                     "naive", "based", "alone", "mono", "plus", "arm"}

# Since 2017 the FDA gives a four-letter suffix to *originator* biologics as
# well (cemiplimab-rwlc, dostarlimab-gxly, tislelizumab-jsgr), so a suffix on
# its own does not mark a biosimilar. Treating every suffix as one blocked the
# originators. A four-letter lowercase suffix is a biosimilar only when it is a
# known biosimilar suffix; a longer or capitalised suffix is a sponsor name.
BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES = {
    # bevacizumab
    "awwb", "bvzr", "maly", "adcd", "tnjn", "nwgd",
    # trastuzumab
    "dkst", "pkrb", "dttb", "qyyp", "anns", "strf",
    # rituximab
    "abbs", "pvvr", "arrx",
    # pegfilgrastim / filgrastim
    "jmdb", "cbqv", "bmez", "apgf", "fpgk", "pbbk", "sndz", "aafi", "ayow",
    # denosumab
    "bbdz", "bmwo", "bnht", "dssb", "kyqq", "nxxp",
}

# Biosimilar brand names. A brand is not caught by any pattern; these were
# found resolving to their originators (Mvasi, Alymsys, Zirabev -> bevacizumab).
BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS = {
    "mvasi", "zirabev", "alymsys", "vegzelma", "avzivi", "jobevne", "aybintio",
    "onbevzi", "oyavas", "abevmy", "byooviz", "kanjinti", "ogivri", "herzuma",
    "ontruzant", "trazimera", "hercessi", "zercepac", "truxima", "ruxience",
    "riabni", "rixathon", "udenyca", "fulphila", "ziextenzo", "nyvepria",
    "fylnetra", "stimufend", "zarxio", "nivestym", "releuko", "granix",
}


# Reformulated products sold under their own name or code. Checked on the
# words of the string, so a brand in brackets ("Paclitaxel (Abraxane)") is seen.
REFORMULATION_NAMES = {
    "abraxane", "abraxis", "doxil", "caelyx", "lipodox", "onivyde", "depocyt",
    "nanopac", "liporaxel", "lipusu", "genexol", "onureg", "cc486",
    "pazenir", "abi007", "apealea", "dn101", "asentar",
}


def is_distinct_product(text: str) -> bool:
    """True when the string names a product that is not the originator."""
    t = (text or "").strip()
    if not t:
        return False
    if RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT.search(t):
        return True
    words = set(re.findall(r"[a-z]+", t.lower()))
    words |= {canonical_key(w) for w in re.findall(r"[A-Za-z]+[\s\-]?\d+", t)}
    if words & (BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | REFORMULATION_NAMES):
        return True
    m = RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX.match(t)
    if not m:
        return False
    suffix = m.group("suffix")
    if suffix.lower() in SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES:
        return False
    # an FDA suffix is four letters, written in either case ("-rwlc", "-RWLC");
    # a title-case word is a sponsor name ("-Pfizer", "-Teva")
    if len(suffix) == 4 and (suffix.islower() or suffix.isupper()):
        return suffix.lower() in BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES
    return True


# An originator's FDA suffix carries no information about the molecule, so it
# is removed before lookup: "cemiplimab-rwlc" -> "cemiplimab".
RE_FDA_SUFFIX = re.compile(
    r"^(?P<inn>[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|ase|grastim|stim|poetin))\s*-\s*(?P<sfx>[A-Za-z]{4})$")


def strip_fda_suffix(text: str) -> str | None:
    m = RE_FDA_SUFFIX.match((text or "").strip())
    if not m:
        return None
    sfx = m.group("sfx")
    if sfx.lower() in SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES or not (sfx.islower() or sfx.isupper()):
        return None
    return m.group("inn")


# "No pembrolizumab", "without bevacizumab": a negated substance is the
# absence of an exposure. Accepting it as CONTAINED_ALIAS recorded the
# opposite of what the arm says.
RE_NEGATED = re.compile(
    r"^\s*(?:no|not|without|none\s+of|absence\s+of|omit(?:ting|ted)?|"
    r"withdrawal\s+of|discontinu(?:e|ed|ation\s+of))\b[\s\-]*", re.I)


# "pembrolizumab-free regimen", "free of steroids": an absence written after
# the substance rather than before it
# ("free base" is a chemical form, not an absence)
RE_NEGATED_AFTER = re.compile(
    r"\b[A-Za-z0-9]+[\s\-]free\b(?![\s\-]*base)|\bfree\s+of\b", re.I)


def is_negated(text: str) -> bool:
    t = text or ""
    return bool(RE_NEGATED.match(t) or RE_NEGATED_AFTER.search(t))

"""Own an unchanged CLI server; independently verify round-3 name and UI cases."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
PORT = 8880
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"


def post(path, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    return {"path": path, "text": text, "status": response.status,
            "body": json.loads(response.read())}


before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port occupied"
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"}
with (OUT / "supplemental_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        inputs = [
            "paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles",
            "paclitaxel with albumin bound formulation",
            "Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid",
            "generic version with bevacizumab",
            "What is the generic version of bevacizumab?",
            "osimertinib, liposomal doxorubicin",
            "liposomal doxorubicin, osimertinib",
            "osimertinib, biosimilar bevacizumab",
            "biosimilar bevacizumab, osimertinib",
        ]
        cases = [post(path, value) for value in inputs
                 for path in ("/api/explore", "/api/resolve")]
        for value in [
            "What trials evaluate paclitaxel with albumin bound nanoparticles?",
            "Which trials evaluate osimertinib, biosimilar bevacizumab?",
            "Which trials evaluate osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?",
            "Which companies sponsor osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?",
        ]:
            cases.append(post("/api/ask", value))
        (OUT / "supplemental_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(
            {"base": BASE, "cases": cases}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
        for case in cases:
            body = case["body"]
            print(json.dumps({"path": case["path"], "text": case["text"], "status": case["status"],
                "center": body.get("center", {}).get("id"), "refused": body.get("refused"),
                "evidence_count": len(body.get("evidence", [])),
                "first_edge": (body.get("evidence") or [{}])[0].get("edge_id")}, ensure_ascii=False))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        (OUT / "supplemental_http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "server_exit": proc.returncode,
            "port_closed": closed, "database_unchanged": before == after,
            "db_sha256_before": before, "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        assert closed and before == after
"""Independent live-server verification of name-linking regression candidates."""
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
BASE = "http://127.0.0.1:8880"

def post(path, text):
    data = json.dumps({"text": text}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8")
    request = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data,
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    return {"path": path, "input": {"text": text}, "status": response.status,
            "elapsed_ms": round((time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000, 3),
            "body": json.loads(response.read())}

inputs = ["bevacizumab - awwb", "89Zr - trastuzumab", "[89Zr] trastuzumab",

          "generic version of bevacizumab", "osimertinib and bevacizumab",
          "[89Zr] trastuzumab and osimertinib",
          "osimertinib and [89Zr] trastuzumab",
          "Mvasi and bevacizumab", "osimertinib EGFR", "EGFR osimertinib"]
before = hashlib.sha256((ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite").read_bytes()).hexdigest()
cases = []
for value in inputs:
    cases.extend([post("/api/explore", value), post("/api/resolve", value)])
for value in ["Which trials evaluate osimertinib and generic version of bevacizumab?",
              "Which trials evaluate [89Zr] trastuzumab and osimertinib?",
              "Which trials evaluate osimertinib and [89Zr] trastuzumab?"]:
    cases.append(post("/api/ask", value))
after = hashlib.sha256((ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite").read_bytes()).hexdigest()
result = {"base": BASE, "cases": cases, "db_sha256_before": before,
          "db_sha256_after": after, "database_unchanged": before == after}
(OUT / "regression_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
for case in cases:

    print(json.dumps({"path": case["path"], "text": case["input"]["text"],
        "status": case["status"], "center": body.get("center", {}).get("id"),
        "refused": body.get("refused"), "also": body.get("also"),
        "components": [{k: c.get(k) for k in ("raw", "status", "drug_id", "verdict")} for c in body.get("components", [])],
        "entities": body.get("entities"), "answer": body.get("answer")}, ensure_ascii=False))
"""Read-only HTTP probes for bfcb83d. Writes only next to this script."""
import concurrent.futures
import hashlib
import json
import time
import urllib.request
import urllib.error
from pathlib import Path

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
BASE = "http://127.0.0.1:8876"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"


def request(payload=None, raw=None, path="/api/explore"):
    data = raw if raw is not None else json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False).encode()
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data,
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as err:
        response = err
    content = response.read()
    try:
        body = json.loads(content)
    except ValueError:
        body = content.decode(errors="replace")
    return {"status": response.status, "bytes": len(content),
            "ms": round((time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000, 3), "body": body}


def digest():
    return hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()


before = digest()
get = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=10)
html = get.read()
(OUT / "served_try.html").write_bytes(html)
results = {"get": {"status": get.status, "bytes": len(html),
                    "matches_worktree": html == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()},
           "cases": {}}

    "wrong_route": {"text": "EGFR"},
